program launch, I couldn’t sit still at work—I was starting new projects, volunteering for extra committees, and signing up for community art events all at once, as if I had to keep doing more and more.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): For a few weeks after the rollout, I caught myself thinking, “I’m really the only one here who knows how to turn this place around,” and I felt oddly certain that I could handle anything without needing much help from anyone.\n- S036 (Decreased_Sleep): In that same high-energy phase, I was going to bed well after midnight, getting maybe four hours of sleep, and still waking up wired and ready to dive into emails or planning another project without feeling tired at all.\n- S037 (Pressured_Speech): My coworkers even joked that I wouldn’t let anyone get a word version of me she’s coming home to.\n- duration: Looking back, these cycles of feeling overly revved up for a few weeks and then sinking into long stretches of feeling low and drained have been happening on and off for at least a few years, but they’ve become especially noticeable since the pressures at home and work ramped up this past year.\n- depressive_episode_duration: This current low As that surge of activity tapered off, she found herself increasingly emotionally raw and self‑critical about minor conflicts with staff and family, canceling hikes and yoga classes and having trouble keeping up with schedules she used to manage despite her relaxed style. verge of tears for no clear reason.\n- S025 (Loss_of_Interest): I used to really look forward to my Saturday hikes and my art class, but over the past few weeks I keep canceling because I just can’t dishwasher feels like it takes everything I’ve got.\n- S030 (Worthlessness_Guilt): I keep going over every little thing I’ve said or done, like snapping at my daughter or missing a deadline, and I end up convinced I’m a terrible mother and a lousy manager who’s letting everyone down, even when people tell me I’m being too hard on myself.\n- S031 (Diminished_Thinking): In meetings and even at home when my husband is talking, my mind just sort of blanks out and I lose the thread of what’s being said, and simple decisions like what to cook for dinner can leave me standing in the kitchen frozen with ideas about new programs, and the next I’d snap at my daughter over something minor and then wonder why I’d overreacted.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): During that energized period after the program launch, I couldn’t sit still at work—I was starting new projects, volunteering for extra committees, and signing up for community art events all at once, as if I had to keep doing more and more.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): For a few weeks after the rollout, I caught myself thinking, “I’m really the only one here who knows how to turn this place around,” and I felt oddly certain that I could handle anything without needing much help from anyone.\n- S036 (Decreased_Sleep): In that same high-energy phase, I was going to bed well after midnight, getting maybe four hours of sleep, and still waking up wired and ready to dive into emails or planning another project without feeling tired at all.\n- S037 (Pressured_Speech): My coworkers even joked that I wouldn’t let anyone get a word in, because in meetings I’d talk fast and jump from one idea to the next without pausing long enough for people to respond.\n- S038 (Racing_Thoughts): Especially during those weeks when I was riding that high, my thoughts felt like they were racing ahead of me—while I was explaining one idea, my brain was already three ideas ahead, and it was hard to slow myself down.\n- S040 (Increased_Goal_Activity): I went from my usual steady pace to suddenly filling my calendar with extra work projects, organizing a neighborhood art night, and trying to be at every school event, happening on and off for at least a few years, but they’ve become especially noticeable since the pressures at home and work ramped up this past year.\n- depressive_episode_duration: This current low stretch has been dragging on for about five months now, starting not long after that busy high I had around the time of the program rollout at work.\n\nRules:\n- If the patient can specifically answer with ideas about new programs, and the next I’d snap at my daughter over something minor and then wonder why I’d overreacted.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): During that energized period after the program launch, I couldn’t sit still at work—I was starting new projects, volunteering for extra committees, and signing up for community art events all at once, as if I had to keep doing more and more.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): For a few weeks after the rollout, I caught myself thinking, “I’m really the only one here who knows how to turn this place around,” and I felt oddly certain that I could handle anything without needing much help from anyone.\n- S036 (Decreased_Sleep): In that same high-energy phase, I was going to bed well after midnight, getting maybe four hours of sleep, and still waking up wired and ready to dive into emails or planning another project without feeling tired at all.\n- S037 (Pressured_Speech): My coworkers even joked that I wouldn’t let anyone get a word in, because in meetings I’d talk fast and jump from one idea to the next without pausing long enough for people to respond.\n- S038 (Racing_Thoughts): Especially during those weeks when I was riding that high, my thoughts felt like they were racing ahead of me—while I was explaining one idea, my brain was already three ideas ahead, and it was hard to slow myself down.\n- S040 (Increased_Goal_Activity): I went from my usual steady pace to suddenly filling my calendar with extra work projects, organizing a neighborhood art night, and trying to be at every school event, deadlines, forgetting meetings, and backing out of plans with friends and family, to the point that my team has had to quietly pick up my slack and my daughter has told me she doesn’t know which version of me she’s coming home to.\n- duration: Looking back, these cycles of feeling overly revved up for a few weeks and then sinking into long stretches of feeling low and drained have been happening on and off for at least a few years, but they’ve become especially noticeable since the pressures at home and work ramped up this past year.\n- depressive_episode_duration: This current low stretch has been dragging on for about five months now, starting not long after that busy high I had around the time of the program rollout at work.\n\n[Behavioral Guidelines]\n- In the very first But I do feel constantly consumed by this overwhelming guilt, replaying my mistakes and feeling absolutely convinced that I'm a terrible mother and manager who has let everyone down, even though people tell me I'm being too hard on myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you also been feeling down or empty most of the day nearly every day, and have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy? If so, how long has that been going on?\n\nPatient: Yes, most days I wake up with a heavy, gray feeling in my chest and feel on the verge of tears over minor things. I've also completely lost interest in my Saturday hikes and art classes, finding them flat and pointless. This current low stretch has been dragging on for about five months now.\n\nDoctor: Before this current 5-month period, have you ever had another episode lasting at least two weeks when you felt similarly depressed and lost interest in things? skip meals without really meaning to, and when I finally weigh myself I see I’ve dropped a few pounds because I just don’t feel hungry or interested in eating.\n- S027 (Sleep_Disturbance): At night I morning.\n- S028 (Psychomotor_Changes): Lately my body feels like it’s moving through molasses—people at work have commented that I walk slower down the hallway and I catch myself just staring at the computer before I can make my fingers start typing an email.\n- S029 (Fatigue): No matter how much I try to rest, I feel exhausted all day, to the point that just getting ready for work or loading the dishwasher feels like it takes everything I’ve got.\n- S030 (Worthlessness_Guilt): I keep going over every little thing I’ve said or done, like snapping at my daughter or missing a deadline, and I end up convinced I’m a terrible mother and a lousy manager who’s letting everyone down, even when people tell me I’m being too hard on myself.\n- S031 (Diminished_Thinking): In meetings and even at home for ten minutes.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): Right before this slump, there was a stretch where I felt unusually upbeat and almost touchy at the same time—one moment I was joking and buzzing with ideas about new programs, and the next I’d snap at my daughter over something minor and then wonder why I’d overreacted.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): During that energized period after the program launch, I couldn’t sit still at work—I was starting new projects, volunteering for extra committees, and signing up for community art events all at once, as if I had to keep doing more and more.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): For a few weeks after the rollout, I caught myself thinking, “I’m really the only one here who knows how to turn this place around,” and I felt oddly certain that I could handle anything without needing much help from anyone.\n- S036 (Decreased_Sleep): In that same high-energy phase, I was going to bed well after midnight, getting maybe four hours of sleep, and still waking up wired and ready to dive into emails or planning another project without feeling tired at all.\n- S037 (Pressured_Speech): My coworkers even joked that I wouldn’t let anyone get a word in, because in meetings I’d talk fast and jump from one idea to the next without pausing long enough for people to respond.\n- S038 (Racing_Thoughts): Especially during those weeks when I was riding that high, my thoughts felt like they were racing ahead of me—while I was explaining one idea, my brain was already three ideas ahead, and it was hard to slow myself down.\n- S040 (Increased_Goal_Activity): I went from my usual steady pace to suddenly filling my calendar with extra work projects, organizing a neighborhood art night, and trying to be at every school event, almost as if I needed to prove I could be everywhere and do everything at once.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- functional_impairment: Over the past several months, I’ve started missing Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight. Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event. Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.  Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).
